I'm just taking care of business, but they insisted I come see you about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your ideas and ways of doing things are better than others?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I do. Lately, it just feels like everybody needs to either get on board with my plans or just get out of my way. \n\nIt's... well, I'll be having a good time, joking around and feeling ten feet tall, and then the next minute I'm snapping at my wife or my boy just over some little question they ask. I just know what needs doing, and it gets frustrating when people drag their feet.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're more important than others because of your abilities or accomplishments?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I'm more important than anybody else. I'm just a guy who works at the mixing plant and tries to take care of his family. I just... I just feel like I'm the only one who can see how things actually need to be done right now. It's not about being better than them, it's just that I know what I'm doing and they don't seem to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special powers or abilities that others don't have?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I don't have any kind of superpowers or magic tricks or anything. I'm just a regular guy who works hard. Lately, it just feels like everybody needs to either get on board with my plans or just get out of my way. \n\nIt's... well, I'll be having a good time, joking around and feeling ten feet tall, and then the next minute I'm snapping at my wife or my boy just over some little question they ask. I just know what needs doing, and it gets frustrating when people drag their feet.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're more important than others because of your abilities or accomplishments?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I'm more important than anybody else. I'm just a guy who works at the mixing plant and tries to take care of his family. I just... I just feel like I'm the only one who can see how things actually need to be done right now. It's not about being better than them, it's just that I know what I'm doing and they don't seem to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special powers or abilities that others don't have?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I don't have any kind of superpowers or magic tricks or anything. I'm just a regular guy who works hard. I'm feeling great, but if people aren't getting on board with what I'm doing, I just want them out of my way. I'm just trying to get through my own list of things to do, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you deserve special treatment or privileges because of your accomplishments or abilities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that at all. I'm just taking care of business, but they insisted I come see you about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your ideas and ways of doing things are better than others?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I do. Lately, it just feels like everybody needs to either get on board with my plans or just get out of my way. \n\nIt's... well, I'll be having a good time, joking around and feeling ten feet tall, and then the next minute I'm snapping at my wife or my boy just over some little question they ask. I just know what needs doing, and it gets frustrating when people drag their feet.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're more important than others because of your abilities or accomplishments?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I'm more important than anybody else. I'm just a guy who works at the mixing plant and tries to take care of his family. I just... I just feel like I'm the only one who can see how things actually need to be done right now. It's not about being better than them, it's just that I know what I'm doing and they don't seem to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have special powers or abilities that others don't have?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I don't have any kind of superpowers or magic tricks or anything. I'm just a regular guy who works hard. Like I said, I just have a lot of energy right now and a clear head for getting things organized. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Well, kind of like I was saying a minute ago, it's a bit of a mix. I do get into these moods where I'm joking around and just feeling ten feet tall. I guess you could call that unusually happy. \n\nBut then, almost in the same breath, I'm snapping at my wife or my boy over some tiny little question. It's like... I'm feeling great, but if people aren't getting on board with what I'm doing, I just want them out of my way. So it's happy, yeah, but it turns into me being pretty short with people real quick.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these mood swings where you feel great one minute and then snap at people the next?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about a month now. Started right around the time we were forced to start packing up and leaving the old house. Since then, it's just been this back and forth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your way of handling situations at work or in your personal life is significantly better than how others would handle them?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I've been working with the grinding and mixing machines for years, so I know how to do my job, but I don't think I'm better than the next guy. I just have my way of doing things. I wouldn't say it's significantly better than how anyone else would handle it. I'm just trying to get through my own list of things to do, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you deserve special treatment or privileges because of your accomplishments or abilities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that at all. I mean... I've worked hard my whole life, but I don't expect anyone to roll out the red carpet for me or anything. \n\nI just put my head down and do what needs doing. I don't need any special treatment, I just... I just want people to let me get on with my day and not get in my way while I'm doing it. But no, I don't think I'm owed anything extra.\n\nDoctor: Are these mood swings causing problems in your relationships with your family or at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they are. I mean... I actually ended up getting pulled from work recently. There was a safety incident, so they took me off the floor. \n\nAnd at home... it's not much better. My wife and the rest of the family, they've basically stopped trusting me with any real decisions. They won't let me handle the money, or the driving, or even the details of this move we're doing. They just said things had gotten too out of hand lately, so now they're trying to manage everything for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these mood swings happen all the time, or are there periods where you feel completely normal?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean... I don't think they happen all the time. But I don't really know what \"normal\" is supposed to feel like anymore. I just know that lately, things have been really up and down, and it's been causing all these problems. I haven't really kept track of whether there are breaks in between or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that after a period of feeling really down or low, you then enter one of these high-energy, joking phases?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they happen in a specific order like that. I haven't noticed a pattern where one always follows the other. Sometimes I'm just down, and sometimes I'm wound up and can't stop moving or talking. But I couldn't tell you if the low always comes first, or the high. It just sort of happens when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your high-energy periods and low periods happen in a cycle, with one always following the other?\n\nPatient: I... I think I just said this, but no, I don't really see a cycle to it. It's not like a pendulum swinging back and forth on a schedule. I just know I have these times where I'm completely drained, and other times where I'm wired and can't stop. But I haven't noticed one always triggering the other.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current mood swings are related to the stress of your move and other life changes?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that either. I mean, moving is stressful, sure, and there have been some changes lately... but I don't know if that's what's causing all this. It's hard to say if the stress brought it on or if it's just happening on its own. I haven't really connected the two in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the stress of the move and other recent life changes are contributing to your mood swings?\n\nPatient: Well, like I just mentioned a minute ago... I really don't know. I mean, we just talked about this, but I honestly couldn't say if the move is what's causing it. The move was a hassle, sure, but it's hard to tell what's from the stress and what's just... happening on its own. I just don't have a better answer for you on that, I'm afraid.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mood swings, going from high energy to low and back again, follow any kind of regular pattern over time?\n\nPatient: I... well, I feel like we're covering the same ground again, Doc. Like I said when you asked about the cycles a few minutes ago, I really don't see a pattern to it. It doesn't happen on a set schedule or anything like that. I just get those high times and those low times, but there's no real rhyme or reason to when they show up that I can point to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

I'm just trying to get through my own list of things to do, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you deserve special treatment or privileges because of your accomplishments or abilities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that at all. I mean... I've worked hard my whole life, but I don't expect anyone to roll out the red carpet for me or anything. \n\nI just put my head down and do what needs doing. I don't need any special treatment, I just... I just want people to let me get on with my day and not get in my way while I'm doing it. But no, I don't think I'm owed anything extra.\n\nDoctor: Are these mood swings causing problems in your relationships with your family or at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they are. I mean... I actually ended up getting pulled from work recently. There was a safety incident, so they took me off the floor. \n\nAnd at home... it's not much better. My wife and the rest of the family, they've basically stopped trusting me with any real decisions. They won't let me handle the money, or the driving, or even the details of this move we're doing. They just said things had gotten too out of hand lately, so now they're trying to manage everything for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these mood swings happen all the time, or are there periods where you feel completely normal?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean... I don't think they happen all the time. But I don't really know what \"normal\" is supposed to feel like anymore. I just know that lately, things have been really up and down, and it's been causing all these problems. I haven't really kept track of whether there are breaks in between or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that after a period of feeling really down or low, you then enter one of these high-energy, joking phases?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they happen in a specific order like that. I haven't noticed a pattern where one always follows the other. Sometimes I'm just down, and sometimes I'm wound up and can't stop moving or talking. But I couldn't tell you if the low always comes first, or the high. It just sort of happens when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your high-energy periods and low periods happen in a cycle, with one always following the other?\n\nPatient: I... I think I just said this, but no, I don't really see a cycle to it. It's not like a pendulum swinging back and forth on a schedule. I just know I have these times where I'm completely drained, and other times where I'm wired and can't stop. But I haven't noticed one always triggering the other.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current mood swings are related to the stress of your move and other life changes?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that either. I mean, moving is stressful, sure, and there have been some changes lately... but I don't know if that's what's causing all this. It's hard to say if the stress brought it on or if it's just happening on its own. I haven't really connected the two in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the stress of the move and other recent life changes are contributing to your mood swings?\n\nPatient: Well, like I just mentioned a minute ago... I really don't know. I mean, we just talked about this, but I honestly couldn't say if the move is what's causing it. The move was a hassle, sure, but it's hard to tell what's from the stress and what's just... happening on its own. I just don't have a better answer for you on that, I'm afraid.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mood swings, going from high energy to low and back again, follow any kind of regular pattern over time?\n\nPatient: I... well, I feel like we're covering the same ground again, Doc. Like I said when you asked about the cycles a few minutes ago, I really don't see a pattern to it. It doesn't happen on a set schedule or anything like that. I just get those high times and those low times, but there's no real rhyme or reason to when they show up that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your high-energy periods always or often follow your low periods?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I haven't really noticed one always coming right after the other like that.
